People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

Hello, Doctor. Nice to meet you. Am I sitting in the right place? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right. 

Um, well, I guess they wanted me to come in because... well, it's a little embarrassing, to be honest. I don't want to be a bother. But sometimes my talking gets a little mixed up. It's probably not a huge deal, just me getting my wires crossed, you know? But I'll try to explain something simple, like about the keys for the building, and then somehow I start talking about the jail... and then the prayer rug... and then the chess pieces. 

I try really hard to be clear, but people tell me they just can't follow what I'm saying. I don't mean to confuse anybody, I really don't. Is that... is that the kind of thing you need to know? I hope I answered your question okay.

Well, um... I guess... sometimes. I mean, it's not a bad thing, really! Mostly I just feel really, really good. Like, super energetic and happy. I feel like I can take on the world, you know? Oh... um... yes, actually. I, uh... I hope that's not too unusual. I really don't want to seem like a complicated case or anything.

Oh... well, um... I guess I have had times like that, yes. I really hope I'm not painting too grim a picture of myself, Doctor. I try so hard to keep my chin up, I really do.

I get so chatty and loud, and I just want to talk to everybody in the building. It's actually kind of nice, feeling that powerful and awake. Oh... yes, I think we touched on that just a moment ago. I hope I didn't confuse things by bringing it up too early! But yes... people definitely notice. 

When I get into those stretches where I feel so... so powerful and up, I guess I'm pretty hard to miss. I'm just so full of energy, laughing and talking really loud to anyone who will listen. Oh... um... well, that's a tricky one to explain, Doctor. I really hope you don't think I'm... well, crazy or anything. I know how it sounds, and I'd hate for you to get the wrong idea about me. I'm usually very level-headed, I promise.

I barely even need to sleep when I get like that, maybe just a couple hours, and I'm still raring to go. \n\nBut, well... I guess I should admit, sometimes if someone tells me to quiet down or questions what I'm doing when I feel that good... I can get a little, um, snappy. I might raise my voice or get upset over something tiny. I always feel terrible about it afterward, I really do. I don't mean to be rude to anyone. Is... is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm not making myself sound like a bad person.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you heard voices or had your speech get mixed up even when your mood was not unusually high or irritable?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... yes, actually. I, uh... I hope that's not too unusual. I really don't want to seem like a complicated case or anything. \n\nBut, well, there have been times... like, whole weeks where my mood was just fine. I wasn't feeling super energetic or loud, and I wasn't feeling down in the dumps either. Just... calm, you know? Just regular. But even then... for, gosh, I guess it was more than two weeks at a time... I still heard the man's voice near the vents. And... well, I also felt pretty sure that some of the people working in the building were, um, making signals about me behind my back. \n\nI kept it to myself, though! I just put my head down and kept sweeping. I try really hard not to let it bother anyone else. Does that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm explaining it right for your notes.\n\nDoctor: When your mood is not unusually high or irritable, do the voices or the feeling that people are making signals about you still happen?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, yes, sir. I really don't want to confuse you or make your job harder. \n\nBut yes, even when I'm just feeling completely regular... not high or snappy or anything... those things still happen. It all sort of started after... um... well, there was a little misunderstanding at the building I clean. Just a mix-up, really! But there was a brief detention because of it. I try not to think about it too much, because I really am a good worker and I don't want you to think I'm a troublemaker or anything like that. But, yes, it's been since right around then. \n\nI've just been trying to manage it the best I can on my own. Is... is that the right kind of timeline you need for your paperwork, Doctor? I hope I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least one week when you felt so unusually energized or high that you were clearly different from your usual self?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, well... I guess there have been times like that, yes. And... well, I also felt pretty sure that some of the people working in the building were, um, making signals about me behind my back. \n\nI kept it to myself, though! I just put my head down and kept sweeping. I try really hard not to let it bother anyone else. Does that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm explaining it right for your notes.\n\nDoctor: When your mood is not unusually high or irritable, do the voices or the feeling that people are making signals about you still happen?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, yes, sir. Like I... well, like I was just saying a second ago. I'm so sorry if I didn't explain it clearly before! I really don't want to confuse you or make your job harder. \n\nBut yes, even when I'm just feeling completely regular... not high or snappy or anything... those things still happen. The voice and the... the signals. I don't really have much else to say about it, if that's okay? I just try my best to ignore it all. I try not to think about it too much, because I really am a good worker and I don't want you to think I'm a troublemaker or anything like that. But, yes, it's been since right around then. \n\nI've just been trying to manage it the best I can on my own. Is... is that the right kind of timeline you need for your paperwork, Doctor? I hope I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least one week when you felt so unusually energized or high that you were clearly different from your usual self?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, well... I feel so good that I'll just be laughing and talking real loud with everyone I see. Just being friendly, mostly! I might shout at them over something really small. \n\nI always feel terrible about it later, though! I really try to be a nice guy. I hope you don't think I'm aggressive or anything. I just... I get a little carried away sometimes. Does that answer your question, Doctor? But sometimes my talking gets a little mixed up. It's probably not a huge deal, just me getting my wires crossed, you know? But I'll try to explain something simple, like about the keys for the building, and then somehow I start talking about the jail... and then the prayer rug... and then the chess pieces. \n\nI try really hard to be clear, but people tell me they just can't follow what I'm saying. I don't mean to confuse anybody, I really don't. Is that... is that the kind of thing you need to know? I hope I answered your question okay.\n\nDoctor: When you talk this way, do you also have trouble understanding other people’s words, or is it mainly your own speech that gets mixed up?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, no, I don't think so. At least, I really hope I'm not misunderstanding people. I try my best to listen very carefully. \n\nWhen people talk to me, it usually makes sense. It's just... when I try to answer back, that's when things get a little jumbled up. Like I said, it's mostly just my own words getting tangled. I'm sorry if that sounds confusing. Am I explaining that okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard or seen things that other people around you did not?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... gee. That's... well, I really don't want you to think badly of me, Doctor. I know how things like this sound, and I promise I'm not trying to be difficult or make a big deal out of nothing. \n\nBut... yes. Sometimes. When I'm at work, cleaning... like if I'm near the vents, or putting my bucket away in the mop closet... I hear a man's voice. He says my name, or... he tells me that I ruined everything. And it's not just a thought, you know? It sounds as real as someone standing right beside me in the room. \n\nI try my best to just ignore it and keep doing my job so I don't cause any trouble for anybody. I hope it's alright that I told you that... I just want to be honest with you. Did I answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods when your mood was very high or very irritable along with needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess... sometimes. I mean, it's not a bad thing, really! Mostly I just feel really, really good. Like, super energetic and happy. I feel like I can take on the world, you know? I get so chatty and loud, and I just want to talk to everybody in the building. It's actually kind of nice, feeling that powerful and awake. I barely even need to sleep when I get like that, maybe just a couple hours, and I'm still raring to go. \n\nBut, well... I guess I should admit, sometimes if someone tells me to quiet down or questions what I'm doing when I feel that good... I can get a little, um, snappy. I might raise my voice or get upset over something tiny. I always feel terrible about it afterward, I really do. I don't mean to be rude to anyone. Is... is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm not making myself sound like a bad person.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you heard voices or had your speech get mixed up even when your mood was not unusually high or irritable?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... yes, actually. I, uh... I hope that's not too unusual. I really don't want to seem like a complicated case or anything. \n\nBut, well, there have been times... like, whole weeks where my mood was just fine. I wasn't feeling super energetic or loud, and I wasn't feeling down in the dumps either. Just... calm, you know? Just regular. But even then... for, gosh, I guess it was more than two weeks at a time... I still heard the man's voice near the vents. And... well, I also felt pretty sure that some of the people working in the building were, um, making signals about me behind my back. \n\nI kept it to myself, though! I just put my head down and kept sweeping. I try really hard not to let it bother anyone else. Does that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm explaining it right for your notes.\n\nDoctor: When your mood is not unusually high or irritable, do the voices or the feeling that people are making signals about you still happen?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, yes, sir. Like I... well, like I was just saying a second ago. I'm so sorry if I didn't explain it clearly before! I really don't want to confuse you or make your job harder. \n\nBut yes, even when I'm just feeling completely regular... not high or snappy or anything... those things still happen. The voice and the... the signals. I don't really have much else to say about it, if that's okay? I just try my best to ignore it all. I hope I'm being helpful... am I doing alright with these questions, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have these problems been going on continuously for at least six months?\n\nPatient: Oh... gosh, six months? Well... um, yes. It's... it's been a bit longer than that, actually. I hope that's alright to say. \n\nI guess... well, if I really think about it, it's been going on for about two years and five months now. It all sort of started after... um... well, there was a little misunderstanding at the building I clean. Just a mix-up, really! But there was a brief detention because of it. I try not to think about it too much, because I really am a good worker and I don't want you to think I'm a troublemaker or anything like that. But, yes, it's been since right around then. \n\nI've just been trying to manage it the best I can on my own. Is... is that the right kind of timeline you need for your paperwork, Doctor? I hope I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least one week when you felt so unusually energized or high that you were clearly different from your usual self?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, well... I guess there have been times like that, yes. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad person or too difficult to handle. \n\nSometimes... well, I'll have these stretches where I just feel... oh, I don't know, really high up, like I have all the power in the world. I feel so good that I'll just be laughing and talking real loud with everyone I see. Just being friendly, mostly! But... well, I have to admit, if someone questions me when I'm like that, or tells me to quiet down... I can get a little snappy. I might shout at them over something really small. \n\nI always feel terrible about it later, though! I really try to be a nice guy. I hope you don't think I'm aggressive or anything. I just... I get a little carried away sometimes. Does that answer your question, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt very depressed or lost interest in almost everything?\n\nPatient: Oh... well, um... I guess I have had times like that, yes. I really hope I'm not painting too grim a picture of myself, Doctor. I try so hard to keep my chin up, I really do. \n\nBut sometimes... well, there are these bad days. It can last a couple of weeks, maybe more. I'll just... I'll sit on the edge of my bed after my morning prayers, and I just feel so... heavy. And empty. It's silly, I know, but I'll sit there feeling like there's just no point in even cleaning myself up or... or going outside. \n\nI always manage to get back to work eventually, though! I'm a very reliable cleaner, I promise you that. I just... I get a little stuck sometimes. Is it okay that I get like that? I'm trying my best to explain it right for you... I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least one week when your mood was so high or irritable that other people clearly noticed you were different?\n\nPatient: Oh... yes, I think we touched on that just a moment ago. I hope I didn't confuse things by bringing it up too early! But yes... people definitely notice. \n\nWhen I get into those stretches where I feel so... so powerful and up, I guess I'm pretty hard to miss. I'm just so full of energy, laughing and talking really loud to anyone who will listen. I just want to share how good I feel, you know? But... well, like I said, if someone tells me to quiet down or looks at me funny, I can get a bit irritable. I might snap at them. I don't mean to cause a scene, I really don't! It's just... a small thing can make me shout before I even realize I'm doing it. \n\nI always try to apologize if I can. I don't want people thinking I'm a difficult person to be around. Does... does that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm explaining it clearly enough for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had hallucinations or the feeling that people were making signals about you for at least two weeks when you were not having any mood symptoms at all?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well, that's a tricky one to explain, Doctor. I really hope you don't think I'm... well, crazy or anything. I know how it sounds, and I'd hate for you to get the wrong idea about me. I'm usually very level-headed, I promise.\n\nBut... yes, I suppose there have been times like that. Even when I'm feeling perfectly fine—not too high, not too low, just having a calm couple of weeks—I still... well, I still hear him. The man in the vents. It's just... he talks sometimes, you know? And during those same calm weeks, I'll still notice the people in the building making little signals to each other about me. \n\nI try to just keep my head down and do my job when that happens. I don't want to cause any trouble or make anyone uncomfortable. It went on for... oh, more than two weeks, I'd say, even when my mood was completely normal. Is... is that the kind of thing you need to know? I'm so sorry if that sounds strange, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt so unusually energized or high that you got into serious trouble or needed help because of it?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... oh dear. I really hate to admit this part, Doctor. I always try to be such a good worker and a good family man, I really do. I hope you won't think less of me. \n\nBut... yes. Sometimes when I get that... that buzzing energy, things get a little out of hand. I've actually lost some of my cleaning jobs because of it. I'd miss my shifts, which just isn't like me at all, I promise! And... well, I've had to stop going to the mosque, and I stay away from family gatherings now. My family and the others, they... they tell me they just can't rely on me when I'm like that, or they say they can't understand what I'm talking about. I don't want to be a bother or upset anyone, so I just keep to myself. \n\nAnd... please don't be alarmed, but... there have been a few times when I guess I wasn't being very safe. It got to the point where... well, some folks had to call for emergency help to come get me. I felt just terrible causing such a big commotion and wasting everyone's time like that. \n\nI'm so embarrassed to even tell you that. I hope I'm not sounding like a bad person... I'm just trying to be completely honest with you. Did I explain that okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you had voices or felt people were making signals about you, but you did not have any mood symptoms at all?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I'm sorry, Doctor, I think you might have asked me something just like this a little bit ago? I probably didn't do a very good job explaining it the first time. I apologize if I confused you, I really want to be helpful. \n\nBut... yes, sir. Like I was saying, there have definitely been calmer weeks. Times when I wasn't feeling especially high, and I wasn't deeply depressed either. Just... normal and steady, you know? But even during those quiet times, for more than two weeks, I still heard the man in the vents. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much... does that answer it better this time? I just want to make sure you have exactly what you need.\n\nDoctor: When you have the voices or feel that people are making signals about you, do those ever happen while your mood is high or depressed?\n\nPatient: Oh... well, yes sir. I really hope you don't think worse of me for saying this... I know it sounds like a lot, and I'd hate for you to think I'm completely losing my mind. But yes, they happen then, too. \n\nLike... when I get into those spells where I'm only sleeping a few hours a night, and I get so worked up making these big, grand plans for the buildings I clean... the voice is still there. I'll hear the man in the vent talking, and... well, I'll feel absolutely sure that the hallway lights are sending warnings about me. \n\nI always try to just focus on my work and my plans so I don't bother anyone with it, though. I try very hard to keep it to myself. Is... is that what you were asking, Doctor? Oh, my... I'm so sorry, Doctor. I must be doing a terrible job explaining myself today. I know you asked me something really similar just a little bit ago, and I feel awful making you ask it again. I promise I'm not trying to be difficult or waste your time.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, dear... I'm so sorry, sir. I feel like I'm just confusing you today, and I really don't mean to. I know I keep saying the same things, and I hope you're not frustrated with me. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.

But, um... no, sir. It's not just when my mood is acting up. Like I was saying before—and I'm sorry to repeat myself—there are those quiet times. Stretches of two weeks or more where I'm not feeling all worked up and high, and I'm not feeling low and sad either. Just... steady. Normal. 

But even then, when my mood is perfectly fine... the man in the vents is still there. I still hear him. And I still feel absolutely sure about the signals from the people in the building. It doesn't go away just because my mood is calm. 

I really hope that makes sense, Doctor. I'm trying my best to explain it right... did I answer that okay for you?

If I think about the last couple of years... most of the time since this all started, I have been in those... those really keyed-up stretches. The times where I know I talk way too much—and I hope I'm not talking your ear off today, I really try to watch it. But I get so driven, like I just cannot slow down no matter what. I end up taking on these massive, impossible cleaning plans at work. I just want the building to be perfect, you know? But yes, those high-energy spells take up the vast majority of the time I've been dealing with all this.

I hope you don't think poorly of me for getting so carried away like that, I really do try to just be a hard worker. Did I answer that right for you, sir? I want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need.

I hope I'm not repeating myself too much... does that answer it better this time?
